"cpu" | "gpu" — which engine ran the case.
"enabled" | "skip"
"ok" | "fail" — whether create_physical_plan succeeds for this query.
#0 and #2 are the stubs the key project and the per-batch join took for slots nothing else filled; #5 is the finish join's.
#161's other half, a FILTER clause, has no query:
#169: a recipe plan is a chain, so its depth is its length, and the C++ verifier refuses one past 1024 at begin_plan — the whole query, before any call.

Both are `CudfProject`, and telling them apart in a recipe of five calls is the difference between reading it and decoding it.
Both are early-exit quantities, so both are absent from every node of every run that drained rather than a zero on each of them.
Both are generic over [`Backend`](crate::executor::Backend), so each backend monomorphizes and nothing on the per-batch path is boxed.
Both are legal: a shuffle's contract is co-location, and every key into one lane satisfies it — nothing above a scatter may depend on how evenly the lanes were loaded.
Both are plan-time: a shape the engine cannot run is refused where the planner can see it, rather than throwing mid-query.
Both are read twice — by the recipe writer for the wire and by the CPU backend for DataFusion — so a case here is about the rule rather than about either reader.
Both backends emit nothing for an empty lane, which is what makes them one engine here: the device's collapse of no handles is a refusal ([#173](../../../llm-wiki/tickets.md)), so a batch invented on this side would be a row the other cannot produce.
Both branches of `recipe::aggregate` reach the same `finalize_project`, so what this pins is the call list: a second call, per batch rather than at done, since an init that finalizes itself has no compaction to wait for.
Both calls end a lane's build phase, and the hold lifts when every lane has: a lane that owed nothing still has to say so, or the probe subtree is held by a lane that will never build.
Both cases below PASS under the naive `workflows.contains("--test {name}")` this replaced, which is the point: without these, a regression back to substring matching is invisible.

Both sides use a Spark-compatible murmur3: - CPU twin: comet `create_murmur3_hashes` (buffer pre-seeded to 42), then pmod. - GPU: our OWN Spark-murmur3 CUDA kernel `peacock::partitioning::spark_partition_ids` (seed=42, per-column left-to-right running-seed, Spark null-skip, UTF-8 bytes), then pmod. cuDF ships only STANDARD murmur3 (proven ≠ Spark by the probe), so we own the hash kernel (Route B) and reuse cudf::partition for the scatter.

It has no default here: the number comes from the same budget rule that sizes loader batches, which is the driver's (T17), and a stand-in default would be a policy nobody chose.

It holds its whole subtree, itself included, and the hold never lifts — so this is where a run ends with lanes not done and queues non-empty.
It ignores the regeneration variables rather than honouring them — a device that can author its own golden proves nothing against it.
It is a backstop against a lowering that emits ranges before the accumulator has them all (#138).
It is a slice of what the sort ordered rather than a top-N heap, so the rows kept are the ones whose keys win and the same run answers the same way twice — which is what an oracle owes, since neither engine's sort promises a tie order.
It is also the output schema where there is no finalize.
It is also where the matrix's cross join is covered: what connects the two intervals has to be one, so the cell has no query of its own here. injected: tpch/nested-limits A merge over state worth merging: the Welford init, both merges and the finalize project.
It is held rather than consumed: which call takes it, and whether it survives that call, is what the recipe says.
It is marked done as it is held, or the hold would stop it reporting and strand its parent — `LIMIT 0` is the case that forces it.
It is not universal — contiguity is the stronger rule, so row groups differing by orders of magnitude inside one file can beat it.

c_custkey rises with the row groups, so a predicate past the first group's maximum leaves the second — and the index it keeps is the file's, not a position in the survivor list.
coalesce is the shape that makes a general rule wrong here, so this does not guess: a function's result is possibly-NULL.
column -> state
cuDF's own rule for a decimal divide gives scale s_l - s_r, which is 0 here; what travels is the scale DataFusion derived.
customer is 150k rows and supplier 10k; a proportional split would hand the most budget to the source already producing the most bytes.
customer is two row groups at this scale, the second one short.
customer projected to c_custkey: the one committed fixture with two row groups (122880 + 27120), and a narrow column to read back.
customer's key column reads several hundred KB at the same threshold, and gets the whole sequence.
end-of-line, or a separator -> a real, whole-name mention
flatc's output, included verbatim: 7,336 lines, and the whole reason `wire/` is drawn where it is.
key (non-float columns, formatted) -> list of the row's Float64 cells.
key = non-numeric cells joined; vals = the numeric cells (as f64) per row.
mark_done_and_fetch and friends consume the executor, so there is nothing left to ask — the slot goes to zero rather than keeping its last figure.
merge_m2 returns its three state columns together, and the line spells them out.
n_name is field 1 of the file schema, and its chunks are what a scan projecting it reads — not a width derived from the type, which is the whole point of measuring the file.
nation reads a couple of KB, under the threshold: one lane, so nothing to merge back and no shuffle to re-land rows the lane never split.

per join, lanes that have yet to leave their build phase

plan_status: does create_physical_plan SUCCEED for this query? Distinct from the `plan` COLUMN, which records whether a query_plan_test exists.
pmod must differ from raw % for negative hashes (the classic mismatch source).
q1-style groups + NULLs (the null-handling is a classic divergence source).
q15 is the aggregate that finalizes on its own: one batch in one lane is already the whole of every group, so the init node carries the finalize and its recipe is the partial plus a finalize project.
q21 is the single-batch probe, where a filtered semi or anti join is one legacy call that hands the build side over rather than copying it — the same join types as q22 and q13, and a different shape.
q22 is the build-side semi family's finish, whose join type is the node's OWN LeftAnti: a key project per batch, then coalesce and that join at done.
q40 is a plain Right outer: probe-local, one call, no finish.
rank -> node, sorted by (height, index): the first set bit is the pick.
region is the build side — DataFusion put the smaller table there and flipped the predicate — and both sides' ordinal 0 is a different column, which is what makes a mix-up visible.
root(0) over two subtrees: 1 -> 2 and 3 -> 4.
scan -> merge -> scatter -> merge -> unload: a shuffle whose lane count is the shape's, so every layout runs the same plan over the same rows.
scatter over merge over scatter: two shuffles live at the same time, which is a different bound from two joins — nothing here is held, so every queue has to be drained by height alone.
tp4 with batching on, which is what makes the small-table threshold bite.
tpch.minimal has no NULLs anywhere, so every column reads as not-nullable here however it is declared — which is the point: the declaration says nothing.
unload <- join <- [coalesce <- merge_sorted <- source(build, 2), emit(4) <- source(probe, 1)] One of everything the counts below distinguish: a join with two children, a partition accumulator with two input lanes, an emitter reading one lane and filling four, and a lane-scoped node on each side of it.
unload <- join <- [coalesce <- source(build), filter <- source(probe)]
unload(0) <- join(1) <- [build 2 <- 3, probe 4 <- 5]
unload(0) <- outer join(1) <- [build 2, probe 3 = inner join <- [build 4, probe 5]]
unload(0) <- outer(1) <- [build 2 = inner join <- [3, 4], probe 5]
what makes it catch the first `live_cpu` query BEFORE the rollout that needs it, rather than during.
xorshift64*, seeded and fixed: the determinism rules apply to tests too.
Σ of every `output_bytes=` value in a `.cpu.txt` body.
Σ of every `peacockdb_cost=` in the text — one per section — which is what the `.cpu.txt` beside it has to account for.
Σ over the accumulators — held whatever the batch size, so it is spent first.
Σ var-length CONTENT bytes across all columns of one batch — the data-dependent term of [`logical_size_from_schema`], and the CPU's spelling of what a device reports as `varlen_content_bytes`.

── the shapes a join cover does not reach ────────────────────────────────── A union executed as an INTERLEAVE at four lanes: output lane p is built from lane p of each branch, which is the whole claim and is invisible at one lane. injected: tpcds/q33 A union that cannot interleave: its two unions carry branches that disagree on lane count, which is what makes them unions rather than interleaves, and the lanes above are their sum. q77 is the shape this claim was written for — 4+1+4 — and it is not here because it is refused rather than untested: one of its Right outers gets a lane whose build side is empty, and what that owes is its probe side padded, which takes a call the recipe does not publish ([#175](../../llm-wiki/tickets.md#t175)). injected: tpcds/q2 Both row-interval lowerings on one root-to-leaf path — the root-adjacent one becoming the unload's skip/fetch and the mid-plan one a limit over the scan — and the only OFFSETs in either corpus.
